Catalogue expansion is stopped. At most ONE experimental claim may be ACTIVE.
Current experimental claims: 0. Sole pre-claim problem review: A3 heat-exchanger
cleaning, CONTEXT_REQUIRED; no defensible residual against a named site baseline.
All other investigations and candidate inventories are PARKED. The original 100
stubs and later N001-N100 batch are different historical record sets; neither is
a set of independent, matched discovery trials. Prior-art checks have varying depth.
Verified novel Garden findings: 0. Measured incremental Garden savings: none.
Comparative discovery performance: UNMEASURED, not a statistically powered failure.
Historical equations, simulations, negative results and assumed financial scenarios
are retained below. They are not current claims, pilot evidence or savings forecasts.

1. OUR MECHANISM

1.1 Garden mapping
10 objects: TIME, SPACE, THING, EVENT, ACTION, AGENCY, RULE, VALUE, CONTEXT, CLAIM.
24 typed relations (instantiate only those valid in a particular scientific domain):
identifies, causes, governs, values, frames, acts, obeys, assesses, contextualizes,
controls, partOf, dependsOn, owns, delegates, references, derivedFrom, equivalentTo,
contradicts, supports, blocks, hasHypothesis, supersedes, conflictsWith, originatesFrom.
Seven forms: CONSTRUCT, CONTRACT, STATE, RELATION, PROCESS, RULE, PROJECTION.
Cross-cutting scope, evidence, dependency, effects, resources, audit and recovery
checks apply. Molecules have no authority or agency in the human sense.

1.2 Projection-Loss Search
Detailed state X; coarse observable Y=A(X); dynamics T. Search for x1,x2 with
A(x1)=A(x2) but A(T(x1)) != A(T(x2)) in a declared domain. This demonstrates
that the projection is not closed for that prediction; it does not prove novelty.

1.3 Necessity-Challenge Search
Given a claimed prerequisite C for outcome Y >= y_min, seek an admissible
counterexample x with NOT C(x) and Y(x)>=y_min, with full mass/energy, cost,
safety and hidden-processing accounting. This tests necessity, not sufficiency.
TRIZ, constraint relaxation and scientific counterexample search are prior art.

1.4 Decision-Sufficiency Test (proposed Garden application of established
value-of-information theory)
A coarse projection Y is decision-sufficient for a stated context if no feasible
additional measurement Z increases expected net optimal decision value materially.
Define V(X,a) in dollars (or a separately typed objective), diagnostic cost C(Z):
Delta_net(Z) = E[max_a E[V(X,a)|Y,Z]]
             - E[max_a E[V(X,a)|Y]] - C(Z).
Under an exact common probabilistic model, the gross information value is
nonnegative, but real fitted predictors may have negative realized net value.
Two states with equal Y but different optimal actions indicate a potential
economic projection gap. Require a blinded held-out estimate of Delta_net,
uncertainty, intervention costs and strong modern comparators.
This is NOT a new mathematical theorem.

1.5 Scientific gates
G0 freeze contemporary baseline; G1 identify a measurable residual or costly
prerequisite; G2 typed equations and units; G3 conservation/identifiability;
G4 synthetic null recovery; G5 strong blind baseline comparison; G6 paper-level
prior art; G7 independent physical test; G8 measured incremental net economic
value; G9 independent admission. UNKNOWN never means PASS. The scientific
research register cannot modify Garden's canonical authority.
OPERATIVE ORDER (section 4.14): lock the real decision/baseline, conduct hostile
paper/patent/practice screening (G6), and establish a specific residual (G1)
BEFORE any new candidate equation (G2). Preregistration precedes fitting and
outcome collection; blinded comparison precedes empirical/economic admission.
G0-G9 are this register's research labels, not the GCSC twelve validity gates.
Historical gate numbering above is preserved for traceability, not execution order.

3. IDEAS GENERATED WITH PRIOR-ART CHECKS

3.1 Method of generation
The hypotheses were constructed by mapping coarse and detailed states and
asking whether a hidden state changes physical evolution or optimal economic
actions. This was NOT an independently executed blind Garden-versus-TRIZ
benchmark. A literature check may disqualify the broad mechanism even when a
narrow industrial use remains worth testing.

3.2 Prior-art dispositions (do not promote to discovery)
- Battery Candidate 006: published electrothermal ageing and spatial current/
  temperature coupling. Scoped source comparison and illustrative dimensional
  contract appear in Section 4.
- A1 second-life battery valuation: application-specific sorting, EIS, ICA/DVA,
  degradation-aware economics and value-of-information are published. Narrow
  fixed-budget diagnostic sequence remains unproven.
- A2 recycled polymer grading: morphology-quality and MFI insufficiency are
  already published. Narrow low-cost process acceptance improvement unproven.
- A3 heat exchanger: flow-compensated fouling diagnosis and optimal experiment
  design are existing approaches. A new low-cost safe perturbation policy is
  only a candidate, novelty unknown.
- Candidate 026: two-body thermal RC memory is established. Synthetic
  model-selection results are engineering checks, not physics discovery.
- Candidates 017, 036, 041, 072, 081, 096: their illustrated underlying
  mathematical mechanisms are established, not new laws.
- External examples: pyrite, suzetrigine, tandem solar, direct lithium
  extraction, personalized gene editing were discovered by others, not Garden.
- Remaining original stubs: paper-by-paper prior-art status NOT REVIEWED.
  Broad-category labels must not be treated as evidence of no prior art.

3.3 Recheck accounting
Original candidates rechecked for identity, duplicates and existing status: 100/100.
Specific prior-art risk flags from known worked examples: 8/100.
Paper-level scoped prior-art investigation of original candidates: Candidate 006.
Paper-level scoped prior-art investigation of active candidates: A1 and A2.
Worldwide novelty certifications: 0.
New Garden-originated physical laws established: 0.
Blind Garden-versus-baseline discovery comparisons: 0.
Physical tests of the active set: 0.
Economically demonstrated incremental savings: 0.

Purpose: close the gap between plausible physical effects and measurable
incremental economic decisions. This section records source-checked negative
novelty findings and predefines tests before collecting experimental outcomes.

13.2 PRIOR-ART CHECK — A1 SECOND-LIFE BATTERY VALUATION
Strong existing work found:
 - 2025 "Data-driven classification of lithium-ion batteries for second-life
   applications", Journal of Energy Storage (ScienceDirect):
   https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S2352152X25027070
   EIS-based rapid classification across FIVE second-life use cases; reported
   average accuracy 92%, independent dataset 90%. Application-aware sorting
   is already published, so it cannot be claimed as Garden-originated.
 - 2025 "Technoeconomic decision support for second-life batteries",
   Applied Energy, DOI https://doi.org/10.1016/j.apenergy.2025.125800
   Integrates degradation and economic decisions across use cases.
 - 2024 "Need of voltage and capacity profile reconstruction in incremental
   capacity analysis for second life batteries from repurposer perspective":
   https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S2352152X24030391
   ICA reconstruction/partial-voltage diagnostics for second-life batteries.
 - 2024 review https://doi.org/10.3389/fchem.2024.1358417
   DVA/ICA and degradation modes already recognized.
 - 2018 "Technical Viability of Battery Second Life: A Study From the
   Ageing Perspective", IEEE, https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/document/8279474/
   First-life history and use case affect second-life performance.
 - 2026 battery pack SOH/DVA measurement robustness:
   https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S2590116826000470
Conclusion: Same SOH with different ageing modes, application-specific
sorting, EIS and ICA/DVA are NOT novel ideas. Proposed six-month vs four-year
lifetimes and $25-$100/kWh valuations are HYPOTHETICAL, not observations.
A narrow frontier hypothesis remains: at a FIXED acquisition/testing budget,
can a cost-aware sequence of partial-voltage measurements improve the
OUT-OF-SAMPLE economic assignment value over a modern EIS-based
application-specific classifier? Worldwide novelty of this precise protocol
UNKNOWN; no guarantee of a positive result.

13.3 A1 GARDEN PHYSICAL AND ECONOMIC CONTRACT
Detailed state X: electrode inventories, loss of lithium inventory, active
material losses, transport/impedance distributions, thermal and usage history.
Coarse projection Y=(usable capacity Q [Ah], DC resistance R [ohm]).
Additional observations Z: partial dQ/dV or dV/dQ features (units explicitly
recorded per sensor protocol), optional EIS spectra, age/cycle history.
Action a: select application (low-rate storage, high-power service, recycle),
or select the next safe diagnostic measurement. Context: cell chemistry,
voltage window, temperature, regulatory and safety limits.
Outcome V(x,a) [USD/cell] is expected NET economic value, incorporating
revenue, remaining service life, failure/warranty risk, testing costs and
recycling value. Uncertainty must be estimated on held-out cells.
An economically sufficient coarse projection at tolerance epsilon [USD/cell]
would satisfy |V(x1,a)-V(x2,a)|<=epsilon for matched Y across a declared
state distribution. This is a research adequacy criterion, not an
assumption that conventional battery markets only use capacity.

Decision rule: a*(y,z)=argmax_a E[V(X,a)|Y=y,Z=z].
Baseline: a0*(y)=argmax_a E[V(X,a)|Y=y].
The Bayes optimal expected value of additional information Z is nonnegative
BEFORE test costs under an exact shared probability model. This is a known
decision-theoretic result, NOT a new physical law. Real fitted predictors
can overfit and make realized incremental value negative.
Primary estimand (USD/cell):
 Delta_net = E[V(X,a*(Y,Z))-V(X,a0*(Y))] - C_diagnostic.
For randomized deployment, also account for opportunity cost and pack
integration constraints. Value of a measurement is NOT the same as a
higher classification accuracy percentage.
Signed hypothesis to freeze: Delta_net > 0 for a declared population and
diagnostic cost threshold. Falsifier: held-out 95% confidence interval does
not support positive Delta_net after multiplicity and selection controls;
do not equate non-significance with proof of zero effect.
Potential ranking-reversal test: identify pairs matched on Q,R whose optimal
application ordering reverses when Z is known. Test whether the reversal
generalizes beyond training data and beyond the existing EIS model.

13.4 A1 EXPERIMENT AND SAFETY PROTOCOL
Pilot: approximately 20 retired cells, single chemistry and traceable source,
used ONLY to check feasibility, sensor repeatability and power/sample-size
estimation; cannot establish market-wide valuation gain.
Confirm capacity, DC resistance, EIS, partial voltage profiles at controlled
temperature and rest time; measure degradation-state proxies independently
where feasible. Randomly assign matched cells to two safe application-duty
protocols; a single cell cannot independently experience both lifetime
trajectories, so use matched randomized groups or validated counterfactual
methods with explicit assumptions. High-power cycling and thermal risk require
a qualified battery laboratory, containment, fire safety and authorization.
Blind the prediction team to eventual outcomes; freeze features and model
selection before lifetime follow-up. Strong comparators: EIS application
classifier, full ICA/DVA plus degradation model, and published economic
decision model. Include training, validation, and fully held-out batches,
chemistry/source transfer, calibration and bootstrap uncertainty.
Primary outcome: realized or properly censored net application value
(USD/cell or USD/kWh) after diagnostic costs. Secondary: lifetime
forecast error, Brier/calibration, misclassification, test minutes/cell.
An independent replication cohort is required before claiming economic value.
Timeline assumption: weeks for initial measurements; 3-9+ months for
accelerated pilot cycling; longer for representative field service life.
No cells purchased or physically tested in this work.

13.5 A2 RECYCLED PLASTICS — PRIOR ART AND NARROW CLAIM
Existing prior art:
 - 2024 Polymer morphology of mechanically recycled HDPE/PP blends:
   https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0032386124003343
 - 2026 NIST, "The anisotropic structure and mechanical behavior of films
   extruded from recycled polypropylene":
   https://www.nist.gov/publications/anisotropic-structure-and-mechanical-behavior-films-extruded-recycled-polypropylene
 - 2026 "Virgin blends do not accurately predict the properties of recycled
   plastics": https://doi.org/10.1016/j.checir.2026.100118
 - 2026 batch variability despite nominal specifications:
   https://doi.org/10.1002/app.70501
Conclusion: polymer contaminant morphology, phase topology, and limitations
of melt-flow-index-only grading are ALREADY KNOWN.
Narrow test: does a rapid, low-cost optical/thermal proxy of domain morphology
improve injection-moulding batch ACCEPT/REJECT economic value at fixed
measurement cost over existing spectroscopy/rheology-based QA?
Compare production scrap, energy and downtime on blinded batches.
No new polymer mechanism established; test remains unexecuted.

13.6 HISTORICAL A3-A5 SECONDARY RESEARCH QUESTIONS — NOT ACTIVE CLAIMS
A3 Heat exchangers: does optimal safe flow-perturbation scheduling improve
net cleaning decisions versus modern flow-compensated fouling observers,
including lost production and sensor costs? Prior art exists.
A4 Motors: does a low-cost distributed state proxy improve pumping-network
total energy/cavitation maintenance decisions versus digital-twin MPC at
equal instrumentation and compute? Modern motor optimization is prior art.
A5 Semiconductors: can a compressed stress-temperature covariance hazard
model predict lot defects nearly as well as modern TCAD/defect statistics
with fewer sensors or lower latency? Existing multiphysics prior art.
All three remain mechanism NOVELTY UNKNOWN and EMPIRICAL NOT TESTED.

13.7 BLIND GARDEN VS NON-GARDEN DISCOVERY BENCHMARK
Freeze a problem statement containing only observable baseline variables,
allowed intervention constraints, and raw training data. Do not reveal
candidate titles, mechanism labels, future publications, or intended solution.
Generate Garden hypotheses using 10 objects, typed relations, seven forms,
projection-loss and necessity-challenge operations. Independently generate
TRIZ/constraint-relaxation and standard system-identification hypotheses
under identical time, compute and evidence budgets. Use independent judges,
held-out outcomes and external novelty screening. Record all proposals,
including failures, and publish falsifiers before unblinding.
Model pretraining may contain historical discoveries, so a hidden-paper
retrospective is not sufficient evidence of frontier novelty.
No blind benchmark has been executed here; no discovery advantage claimed.

13.8 ECONOMIC SCENARIOS, NOT ESTIMATED MARKET BENEFITS
For a hypothetical operator processing 100 MWh/year = 100,000 kWh/year,
a genuinely demonstrated +$10/kWh NET assignment gain would equal
$1,000,000/year. This is a multiplication example, not a forecast.
For 20 test cells at $50/kWh and 50 kWh total, nominal cell acquisition
would be $2,500 before transport, equipment, labour, degradation testing
and safety; the earlier "$30k" pilot estimate is not a verified quotation.
Do not infer a global dollar market from this pilot. Calculate realized
value using actual throughput, acquisition prices, failure costs, adoption
and competing reuse/recycling opportunities.

4.2 Mathematical examples from original register — PEDAGOGY, NOT DISCOVERIES
3.1 TEN WORKED MATHEMATICAL EXAMPLES
The following are conditional derivations; most reproduce established mathematics.


WORKED 002 — Battery electrochemistry: electrolyte concentration variance
Model/assumptions: For local electrolyte concentration c(x), let i(x)=i0 exp(-a/c(x)) for c>0, a>0 (illustrative transport-limited constitutive law, not universal Butler–Volmer). Aggregate I=E[i(c)].
Derived result: Taylor expansion gives I-i(E[c]) = (1/2)i''(cbar)Var(c)+O(E|c-cbar|^3), with i''(c)=i(c)*a*(a-2c)/c^4. Thus the leading correction is POSITIVE if cbar<a/2 and NEGATIVE if cbar>a/2, for sufficiently small variance.
Discriminating experiment: Two otherwise matched cells with the same mean c but different measured concentration variance should show the predicted sign change when a/cbar crosses 2. Reject if sign fails after independent measurement of a and confound controls.
Standing: derivation under stated illustrative assumptions; global novelty not established; no real-world test performed.

WORKED 006 — Battery degradation: local heat-current covariance
Model/assumptions: Assume local degradation r(x)=k exp[-E/(R T(x))] j(x)^p, T>0, j>0, k>0, E>0; compare E[r] with r(E[T],E[j]).
Derived result: The second-order correction includes r_Tj Cov(T,j), where r_Tj=r*E*p/(R*T^2*j)>0 for p>0. Thus POSITIVE temperature-current covariance raises the leading-order degradation rate, all else equal; other variance terms can counteract it.
Discriminating experiment: With independently measured T(x), j(x), compare matched-mean cells having different Cov(T,j); preregister a positive partial contribution, not an unconditional net effect.
Standing: derivation under stated illustrative assumptions; global novelty not established; no real-world test performed.

WORKED 017 — Chemical kinetics: barrier-distribution curvature
Model/assumptions: For parallel pathways k(T)=sum_j A_j exp[-E_j/(R T)], A_j>0 and constant E_j, set u=1/T.
Derived result: d² ln k / du² = Var_w(E)/R² >=0, with weights w_j=k_j/k. Strict curvature occurs if at least two contributing barriers differ.
Discriminating experiment: A measured negative curvature of ln k versus 1/T beyond uncertainty falsifies this restricted parallel-Arrhenius class; compare against known variable-prefactor and changing-mechanism alternatives.
Standing: derivation under stated illustrative assumptions; global novelty not established; no real-world test performed.

WORKED 026 — Heat transfer: interface thermal memory
Model/assumptions: Two lumped thermal bodies exchange heat with interfacial conductance h and temperatures T1,T2: C1*T1'=Q1-h(T1-T2); C2*T2'=Q2+h(T1-T2).
Derived result: Eliminating T2 produces a convolution-memory contribution to T1 with characteristic timescale tau=C2/h (when Q2=0 and coefficients constant); an instantaneous single-temperature model cannot generally reproduce the same transient for different T2(0).
Discriminating experiment: Prepare equal T1(0) but different T2(0); predict different initial slopes by -h*Delta(T1-T2)/C1. Known thermal RC mathematics; novelty requires better identification or new domain-specific limit.
Standing: derivation under stated illustrative assumptions; global novelty not established; no real-world test performed.

WORKED 036 — Fracture mechanics: overload sequence memory
Model/assumptions: For a simple crack-length model da/dN=C[DeltaK(a)]^m g(z), add a measured closure state z with dz/dN=-(z-z_eq(DeltaK))/n0; C>0, n0>0.
Derived result: At identical crack length and present DeltaK, histories with distinct z imply different da/dN. As n0->0, z tracks z_eq and the reduced baseline is recovered if g(z_eq)=1 under the chosen calibration.
Discriminating experiment: Compare overload-first and no-overload histories at matched present DeltaK and crack length; preregister the direction only after calibrating the closure-state sign. Load-sequence effects are established; no global novelty claim.
Standing: derivation under stated illustrative assumptions; global novelty not established; no real-world test performed.

WORKED 041 — Turbulence: intermittency-conditioned closure
Model/assumptions: Let q be local turbulent kinetic energy flux and y its coarse state. Introduce a measured intermittency indicator chi and model E[q|y,chi] rather than E[q|y].
Derived result: By the law of total variance, Var(q|y)=E[Var(q|y,chi)|y]+Var(E[q|y,chi]|y); conditioning cannot increase the optimal squared-error Bayes prediction risk in expectation.
Discriminating experiment: On held-out flow regimes, test whether measured chi yields nonzero conditional-mean variance and improves flux prediction at matched complexity; this is an information-theoretic guarantee, not a new turbulence law.
Standing: derivation under stated illustrative assumptions; global novelty not established; no real-world test performed.

WORKED 063 — Pharmacokinetics: organ perfusion covariance
Model/assumptions: Let organ extraction flux be F(Q,C)=Q*C*E(Q) with variable perfusion Q and extraction E(Q), C fixed in a short window.
Derived result: E[F(Q,C)]-F(E[Q],C) ~= (C/2) [2 E'(Qbar)+Qbar E''(Qbar)] Var(Q); the sign depends on the extraction law, not on 'heterogeneity' alone.
Discriminating experiment: Measure Q distribution and extraction curve independently, then preregister the predicted signed correction; reject if matched-mean experiments disagree.
Standing: derivation under stated illustrative assumptions; global novelty not established; no real-world test performed.

WORKED 072 — Epidemiology: infection-age infectiousness
Model/assumptions: Use infection-age density i(t,a) and infectivity beta(a). Incidence J(t)=S(t)/N * integral beta(a)i(t,a) da.
Derived result: The aggregate SIR incidence beta_bar*S*I/N is exact at an instant only if beta_bar equals the current infection-age weighted mean. Equal I can imply different J for different age distributions.
Discriminating experiment: Compare cohorts matched on S,I,N but different measured infection-age distributions; predict incidence from beta(a) fixed on training data. Age-structured models are established.
Standing: derivation under stated illustrative assumptions; global novelty not established; no real-world test performed.
WORKED 081 — Climate dynamics: ocean vertical heat memory
Model/assumptions: Two-box ocean heat model: C_s*T_s'=F-lambda*T_s-k(T_s-T_d); C_d*T_d'=k(T_s-T_d).
Derived result: At fixed T_s and forcing F, the surface warming rate differs by (k/C_s)*Delta T_d across histories. A one-box model based only on current T_s cannot be exact for both.
Discriminating experiment: Compare historical forcings that produce matched surface temperature but different subsurface heat content; test out-of-sample surface response. Two-box climate models are established.
Standing: derivation under stated illustrative assumptions; global novelty not established; no real-world test performed.

WORKED 096 — Optical materials: nano-inclusion spatial correlation
Model/assumptions: For dilute scatterers with positions r_i and single-scatterer amplitude f(q), total amplitude A(q)=f(q)*sum_i exp(i q.r_i).
Derived result: E[|A(q)|²]=|f(q)|² [N + sum_(i!=j) E exp(i q.(r_i-r_j))]; identical number density but different pair correlations yield different intensity.
Discriminating experiment: Fabricate matched-density samples with different pair correlations; preregister scattering contrast at chosen q. This is established structure-factor physics.
Standing: derivation under stated illustrative assumptions; global novelty not established; no real-world test performed.




4.3 Candidate 006 prior art and dimensional contract
3.6 COMPLETED SCOPED LITERATURE SCREEN + THEORY EQUATION CONTRACT — CANDIDATE 006
Date: 2026-10-09. Scope: targeted published literature review, NOT exhaustive global novelty certification.

RESEARCH QUESTION: Can the joint spatial temperature-current distribution be compressed
into a low-cost observable that improves battery ageing prediction relative to modern
distributed electrothermal-degradation models, rather than relative to a lumped model?

PRIOR ART FOUND (mechanism already established):
[1] Effect of thermal gradients on inhomogeneous degradation in lithium-ion batteries,
Communications Engineering (2024). https://www.nature.com/articles/s44172-023-00124-w
Distributed electrothermal-degradation model incorporates spatial T, current, resistance
and degradation; reports strong degradation acceleration from modest thermal gradients.
[2] Understanding calendar aging degradation in cylindrical lithium-ion cell: A novel
pseudo-4-dimensional electrochemical-thermal model, Applied Energy 377 (2025), 124640.
https://doi.org/10.1016/j.apenergy.2024.124640
Models local current distributions, lithium heterogeneity and interacting ageing pathways.
[3] Modeling the internal inhomogeneous aging behavior within individual Li-ion cells
— During cycle aging, Journal of Energy Storage 86 (2024), 111186.
https://doi.org/10.1016/j.est.2024.111186
Models and experiments on temperature/potential gradients and spatially heterogeneous ageing.
[4] Degradation mechanism of lithium-ion battery under appropriate in-plane temperature
gradient (2025). https://doi.org/10.1016/j.geits.2025.100352
Examines alignment of temperature and current-density gradients and charging degradation.

NOVELTY DISPOSITION: Coupled local T/current ageing and spatial matching are NOT NOVEL.
A compact covariance-based reduced-order surrogate might still be useful, but its
novelty and predictive advantage remain UNKNOWN. No claim of priority or patentability.

THEORY EQUATION CONTRACT (illustrative constitutive law, NOT universal SEI physics):
Let r(x,t)=k exp[-E/(R T(x,t))] (j(x,t)/j_ref)^p.
T [K]>0; j and j_ref [A/m^2]>0; E [J/mol]; R [J/(mol K)];
p dimensionless; k [damage-unit/time]. Thus r [damage-unit/time].
Spatial average D(t)=<r(x,t)>; lumped D0=r(Tbar,jbar).
Let a=E/R [K]. At small bounded fluctuations about Tbar,jbar:
D-D0 = 0.5*r_TT Var(T) + r_Tj Cov(T,j)
      + 0.5*r_jj Var(j) + third-order remainder.
r_TT=r*(a^2/T^4-2a/T^3) [damage/(time K^2)].
r_Tj=r*a*p/(T^2*j) [damage/(time K (A/m^2))].
r_jj=r*p*(p-1)/j^2 [damage/(time (A/m^2)^2)].
Each product with its variance/covariance has units [damage-unit/time].
For p>0, a>0, positive Cov(T,j) contributes positively to the SECOND-ORDER
correction, but the TOTAL correction can have either sign depending on
Var(T), Var(j), p, the domain and higher-order terms.
The constant-temperature/current limit recovers the lumped baseline exactly.
The approximation is not reliable for large gradients or near j=0.

IDENTIFIABILITY AND FALSIFIERS:
Measure local T(x,t) and j(x,t) independently; fit k,E,p on training cells.
Use physically consistent field measurement, positive j, fixed geometry and chemistry.
Pre-register primary endpoint: held-out capacity-loss prediction error over cycles,
not simply statistical significance of a fitted covariance coefficient.
Test whether the low-order covariance surrogate meets a specified accuracy budget
versus modern distributed models [1]-[4], and a reduced-order model with the same
parameter count and sensor budget. Include spatially shuffled counterfactuals,
matched means, distinct covariance, time extrapolation, confidence intervals,
sensor noise, current reversals and cross-cell replication.
Reject the reduced model if it fails the held-out accuracy/cost trade-off.
For scientific novelty, identify an improvement not already taught in [1]-[4]
or later literature; this has NOT been done.

GARDEN GRAPH INSTANCE:
THING=cell/electrode region; TIME=cycle history; SPACE=electrode coordinates;
EVENT=damage increments; ACTION=controlled charge waveform;
AGENCY=experimenter/controller only; RULE=conservation and kinetic contract;
VALUE=forecast error and cost; CONTEXT=chemistry/geometry/cooling;
CLAIM=covariance surrogate improves efficiency at fixed accuracy.
partOf(cell region, cell), dependsOn(local ageing, T and j),
derivedFrom(correction, Taylor expansion), contextualizes(chemistry, claim),
supports/contradicts(measurements, claim). Causal attribution requires experiments.
Seven forms: CONSTRUCT=cell model; CONTRACT=equation above; STATE=T,j,damage;
RELATION=typed physical dependencies; PROCESS=cycling and degradation;
RULE=dimensional and limiting constraints; PROJECTION=spatial field to D(t).

STATUS: DIMENSIONAL ANALYSIS PASS for illustrative law; ANALYTICAL TAYLOR RESULT
PASS under smoothness/small-fluctuation assumptions; REAL BATTERY EQUATION
IDENTIFICATION UNKNOWN; FULL LITERATURE NOVELTY UNKNOWN; EMPIRICAL TEST NOT RUN;
BENEFIT NOT QUANTIFIED; SCIENTIFIC DISCOVERY NOT ESTABLISHED.

4.6 External published scientific examples and narrow fouling candidate
10. REAL-WORLD SCIENTIFIC BREAKTHROUGHS — VERIFIED SOURCE-SCOPED EXAMPLES
These are external scientific achievements, NOT discoveries produced by Garden.
Their inclusion demonstrates how Garden could represent and search analogous
opportunities. No verified absence of prior art is claimed.

10.1 Semiconductor-quality pyrite from commercial iron ore (2026)
Source: Lee et al., Physical Review Applied 26, 024033, published 13 Aug 2026.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1103/6twd-lvvg
Researchers converted impurity-rich commercial iron ores, including taconite,
into high-quality FeS2 crystals without a separate conventional precursor
purification stage. IMPORTANT: the paper identifies TWO synergistic effects:
few impurities efficiently dope FeS2, AND reduction/sulfidation/crystal-growth
steps themselves introduce substantial purification. Thus the claim "dirty
ore works because pyrite tolerates every impurity" is FALSE.
Necessity challenge: ultra-high-purity starting ore is not universally
necessary for semiconductor-quality FeS2 under this process.
Economic hypothesis: lower feedstock purification cost if yields, performance,
process energy, crystal growth scale and lifetime compete with existing routes.
Validation: impurity mass balance, device metrics, pilot cost accounting.
Evidence: published laboratory materials result; industrial economic gain UNKNOWN.

10.2 Suzetrigine / JOURNAVX (2025)
Source: US FDA approval 30 Jan 2025:
https://www.fda.gov/news-events/press-announcements/fda-approves-novel-non-opioid-treatment-moderate-severe-acute-pain
First approved medicine in a new non-opioid acute-pain class; targets peripheral
NaV1.8 pain signalling. Two pivotal trials showed superiority to placebo.
Do NOT infer it is categorically superior to opioids, completely without
side effects, effective for all chronic pain, or proven to eliminate addiction
at population scale. Approval is for moderate-to-severe acute pain in adults.
Necessity challenge: central opioid-receptor engagement is not necessary for
clinically useful acute analgesia.
Economic hypothesis: value from an additional non-opioid option and reduced
opioid exposure, subject to real comparative efficacy, pricing and adoption.
Evidence: regulatory approval; population-scale economic impact unmeasured here.

10.3 Perovskite–silicon tandem photovoltaics (2024–2026)
Sources:
https://www.oxfordpv.com/press-releases/oxford-pv-solar-technology-patent
https://www.pv-magazine.com/2026/07/15/longi-sets-new-world-record-with-35-5-efficient-perovskite-silicon-tandem-cell/
https://www.pv-magazine.com/2026/01/16/oxford-pv-targets-20-year-lifetime-for-perovskite-silicon-tandem-modules-by-2028/
A 35.5% certified 2026 result concerns a laboratory tandem CELL, not a
mass-produced MODULE. Oxford PV's reported commercial module efficiency was
around 25%, and its 2026 roadmap targeted mass production expansion in 2027.
Commercial first shipments began in 2024; full industry-wide scale is not
established by a laboratory efficiency record.
Necessity challenge: single-junction silicon is not the only feasible
architecture compatible with much of the silicon manufacturing ecosystem.
Economic test: lifetime energy yield and LCOE, not cell efficiency alone.
Evidence: lab records and early commercial deployments; universal 34% modules
or proven massive cost reductions NOT established.

10.4 Direct lithium extraction from brines (2025–2026)
Sources:
https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1537137/000117184326002638/exh_991.htm
https://www.evove.tech/dle-onsite-demo/
Industrial-scale demonstrations have processed substantial brine volumes, but
the commercial maturity, recovery and costs depend on brine chemistry and
technology. Do not infer that every DLE method processes any brine in hours,
or that demonstrations establish universal commercial profitability.
Necessity challenge: months-long solar evaporation is not a universal
prerequisite for extracting lithium from brines.
Economic test: lithium recovery, selectivity, water/chemical/energy demand,
sorbent degradation, capex, opex and commercial uptime.
Evidence: operating demonstrations; general global cost savings UNKNOWN.

10.5 Patient-specific gene editing for CPS1 deficiency (2025)
Sources:
https://www.nih.gov/news-events/news-releases/infant-rare-incurable-disease-first-successfully-receive-personalized-gene-therapy-treatment
https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12713542/
One infant received individualized in-vivo base-editing therapy and showed
encouraging early clinical responses. The study explicitly requires longer
follow-up to establish durability and safety. This is not a generally approved
personalized-CRISPR treatment for all rare disorders.
Necessity challenge: a mass-market one-size-fits-all therapy is not the only
possible development pathway for some rare variants.
Economic test: platform reuse, regulatory and manufacturing costs, outcomes
and equitable access; costs per patient remain unknown here.
Evidence: pioneering initial clinical result, not established scalable cure.

11. HISTORICAL FOULING-IDENTIFICATION EXAMPLE — PEDAGOGY, NOT AN ACTIVE CLAIM
Current disposition: PARKED_KNOWN_OVERLAP; see the decision-focused screen in 4.14.
The following original derivation and limitations are retained as historical text.
Status: mathematical identification under assumptions; NOT a novel physical law.
Problem: infer a heat exchanger's flow-independent fouling resistance Rf while
convection changes with flow. Assume the simplified model
    R(q) = Rf + A*q^(-n),  q>0, n>0 fixed and independently calibrated.
Two stable flow points q1 != q2 imply
    Rf = [R(q1)*q2^(-n) - R(q2)*q1^(-n)] /
         [q2^(-n) - q1^(-n)].
Dimensions: R and Rf are thermal resistance [K/W] for a specified device,
A has [K/W] * [flow]^n. Formula is exact within the stated model, but
poorly conditioned if q1 and q2 are too close; flow perturbations can
change fouling or process state, invalidating the assumptions.
Research extension: choose safe perturbation pairs to minimize uncertainty
of Rf under a sensor-noise covariance model, while minimizing downtime and
process energy. Compare against published flow-compensated thermal/hydraulic
fouling diagnosis, adaptive observers and optimal experiment design.
Prior art exists for flow-compensated fouling estimates and experiment design;
worldwide novelty of this narrow combination is UNKNOWN.
Economic benefit if validated: lower unnecessary cleaning and recovered heat
transfer, conditional on avoided maintenance costs exceeding test disruption.
Falsifier: out-of-sample resistance estimates and maintenance decisions fail
to improve on a matched modern comparator.
Status: UNVERIFIED research candidate, no new empirical data.



4.7 Reproducible Candidate 026 synthetic evidence
Original ARX comparator: 3/100 false null selections, 94/100 two-body detections
after independent null calibration, under the specified synthetic conditions.
Strong physical output-error comparator: 0/20 null, 20/20 hidden at noise .012;
0/12 null, 12/12 hidden at noise .024. Candidate structures were supplied,
so this does not establish blind scientific discovery. Both scripts are embedded
below. Raw JSON dumps were removed as redundant; original data remain in
Git history.
--- BEGIN EMBEDDED garden_candidate026_harness.py ---
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Reproducible candidate-026 synthetic closure experiment; no empirical/novelty claim."""
import json
import numpy as np
from scipy.linalg import expm

DT=0.25
TRAIN=320
TEST=160

def trajectory(seed, two_body, sigma=0.012):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n=TRAIN+TEST+2
    u=np.zeros(n)
    for t in range(n):
        u[t]=1.2*np.sin(0.073*t)+0.6*np.sin(0.019*t+0.7)+(0.8 if (t//29)%2 else -0.4)+0.2*rng.normal()
    if two_body:
        C1,C2,h,g=2.0,4.0,0.9,0.24
        # C1 dT1/dt = u - g*T1 - h*(T1-T2); C2 dT2/dt = h*(T1-T2)
        A=np.array([[-(g+h)/C1,h/C1],[h/C2,-h/C2]])
        B=np.array([[1/C1],[0.]])
        M=np.zeros((3,3));M[:2,:2]=A;M[:2,2:]=B
        E=expm(M*DT);Ad=E[:2,:2];Bd=E[:2,2]
        x=np.array([0.1,-0.6]); y=[]
        for k in range(n):
            y.append(x[0]);x=Ad@x+Bd*u[k]
    else:
        C,g=2.0,0.35
        a=np.exp(-g/C*DT);b=(1-a)/g
        x=0.1;y=[]
        for k in range(n):
            y.append(x);x=a*x+b*u[k]
    return np.asarray(y)+sigma*rng.normal(size=n),u

def matrices(y,u,p):
    # ARX(p,p) uses current and p-1 previous y and u to predict next sample
    X=[];z=[];ts=[]
    for t in range(p-1,len(y)-1):
        X.append([*([y[t-j] for j in range(p)]),*([u[t-j] for j in range(p)]),1.])
        z.append(y[t+1]);ts.append(t)
    return np.array(X),np.array(z),np.array(ts)

def evaluate(y,u,p):
    X,z,t=matrices(y,u,p)
    tr=t<TRAIN-1;te=t>=TRAIN
    # Fixed ridge is part of the preregistered model, not tuned on test.
    lam=1e-5
    L=np.eye(X.shape[1]);L[-1,-1]=0
    beta=np.linalg.solve(X[tr].T@X[tr]+lam*L,X[tr].T@z[tr])
    pred=X@beta
    train_mse=np.mean((z[tr]-pred[tr])**2)
    test_mse=np.mean((z[te]-pred[te])**2)
    # Training BIC with parameter penalty
    bic=tr.sum()*np.log(max(train_mse,1e-15))+X.shape[1]*np.log(tr.sum())
    return dict(train_mse=float(train_mse),test_mse=float(test_mse),bic=float(bic),coefficients=beta.tolist())

def run():
    # Calibrate the threshold on independent one-body null simulations; measurement noise can make ARX2 look better even when no hidden physical state exists.
    null_calibration=[]
    for seed in range(100):
        yc,uc=trajectory(seed+5000,False)
        bc=evaluate(yc,uc,1);ec=evaluate(yc,uc,2)
        null_calibration.append(1-ec['test_mse']/bc['test_mse'])
    null_threshold=float(np.quantile(null_calibration,0.95))
    allout={}
    for label,two in [('null_one_body',False),('hidden_two_body',True)]:
        entries=[]
        for seed in range(100):
            y,u=trajectory(seed+1000,two)
            b=evaluate(y,u,1);e=evaluate(y,u,2)
            improvement=1-e['test_mse']/b['test_mse']
            # require >= 10% test improvement AND BIC improvement of at least 10
            selected=improvement>null_threshold and e['bic']<=b['bic']-10
            entries.append({'seed':seed,'baseline_test_mse':b['test_mse'],'extension_test_mse':e['test_mse'],'fractional_improvement':improvement,'selected':selected,'delta_bic':e['bic']-b['bic']})
        allout[label]={'n':len(entries),'selection_rate':sum(x['selected'] for x in entries)/len(entries),'median_test_improvement':float(np.median([x['fractional_improvement'] for x in entries])),'median_baseline_mse':float(np.median([x['baseline_test_mse'] for x in entries])),'median_extension_mse':float(np.median([x['extension_test_mse'] for x in entries])),'cases':entries}
    allout['design']={'null_calibration_quantile':0.95,'null_calibration_threshold':null_threshold,'calibration_seeds':100,'dt':DT,'train_steps':TRAIN,'test_steps':TEST,'noise_sd':0.012,'seeds_per_condition':100,'comparison':'ARX(1,1) vs ARX(2,2) with equal train/test sequences','selection_rule':'heldout MSE improvement above independent null 95th percentile AND training BIC decreases by >=10','scope':'synthetic only; comparator is not strongest modern system identification; no novelty claim'}
    return allout
if __name__=='__main__':
    r=run()
    with open('/mnt/data/garden_candidate026_harness_results.json','w') as f:json.dump(r,f,indent=2)
    print('calibrated_null_threshold=',round(r['design']['null_calibration_threshold'],4))
    for key in ['null_one_body','hidden_two_body']:
        a=r[key];print(key,'n=',a['n'],'selection=',a['selection_rate'],'median_gain=',round(a['median_test_improvement'],4),'baseline_mse=',round(a['median_baseline_mse'],7),'extended_mse=',round(a['median_extension_mse'],7))
--- END EMBEDDED garden_candidate026_harness.py ---

--- BEGIN EMBEDDED garden_candidate026_strong_comparator.py ---
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Noise-aware output-error thermal RC benchmark; synthetic research only."""
import argparse, json
import numpy as np
from scipy.linalg import expm
from scipy.optimize import least_squares
from garden_candidate026_harness import trajectory, TRAIN, TEST, DT

def simulate(u, p, order):
    # Parameters positive, initial temperatures unrestricted; zero-order-held forcing.
    if order == 1:
        C,g=np.exp(p[:2]); x=p[2]; a=np.exp(-g/C*DT); b=-np.expm1(-g/C*DT)/g
        y=np.empty(len(u))
        for t,ut in enumerate(u):
            y[t]=x;x=a*x+b*ut
        return y
    C1,C2,h,g=np.exp(p[:4]); x=np.array(p[4:6],float)
    A=np.array([[-(g+h)/C1,h/C1],[h/C2,-h/C2]])
    B=np.array([[1/C1],[0.]])
    M=np.zeros((3,3));M[:2,:2]=A;M[:2,2:]=B
    E=expm(M*DT);ad=E[:2,:2];bd=E[:2,2]
    y=np.empty(len(u))
    for t,ut in enumerate(u):
        y[t]=x[0];x=ad@x+bd*ut
    return y

def fit(y,u,order):
    starts=([np.log(2),np.log(.35),float(y[0])],) if order==1 else ([np.log(2),np.log(4),np.log(.9),np.log(.24),float(y[0]),float(y[0])],)
    best=None
    for start in starts:
        start=np.array(start)
        nphys=2 if order==1 else 4
        lo=np.r_[np.full(nphys,-5.),np.full(order,-20.)]
        hi=np.r_[np.full(nphys,5.),np.full(order,20.)]
        result=least_squares(lambda p:simulate(u[:TRAIN],p,order)-y[:TRAIN],start,bounds=(lo,hi),max_nfev=250,ftol=1e-8,xtol=1e-8,gtol=1e-8)
        sse=float(np.sum(result.fun**2))
        if best is None or sse<best[0]:best=(sse,result.x,result.success)
    sse,p,success=best
    full=simulate(u,p,order)
    test_mse=float(np.mean((full[TRAIN:TRAIN+TEST]-y[TRAIN:TRAIN+TEST])**2))
    bic=TRAIN*np.log(max(sse/TRAIN,1e-15))+len(p)*np.log(TRAIN)
    return dict(bic=float(bic),test_mse=test_mse,fit_success=bool(success),params=p.tolist())

def run(n,noise,seed_start):
    out={'settings':{'n_per_class':n,'noise_sd':noise,'seed_start':seed_start,'selection_rule':'training BIC improvement >=10; output-error model, holdout reported independently','scope':'synthetic, structure-aware comparator; not blind discovery'}}
    for label,two in [('null_one_body',False),('hidden_two_body',True)]:        cases=[]
        for k in range(n):
            y,u=trajectory(seed_start+k+(100000 if two else 0),two,sigma=noise)
            a=fit(y,u,1);b=fit(y,u,2)
            cases.append({'seed':k,'selected_two_body':b['bic']<=a['bic']-10,'delta_bic':b['bic']-a['bic'],'one_body_test_mse':a['test_mse'],'two_body_test_mse':b['test_mse'],'fit_success':a['fit_success'] and b['fit_success']})
        out[label]={'n':n,'selected':sum(c['selected_two_body'] for c in cases),'selection_rate':float(np.mean([c['selected_two_body'] for c in cases])),'median_one_body_test_mse':float(np.median([c['one_body_test_mse'] for c in cases])),'median_two_body_test_mse':float(np.median([c['two_body_test_mse'] for c in cases])),'cases':cases}
    return out
if __name__=='__main__':
    ap=argparse.ArgumentParser();ap.add_argument('--n',type=int,default=20);ap.add_argument('--noise',type=float,default=.012);ap.add_argument('--seed',type=int,default=9100);ap.add_argument('--output',default='garden_candidate026_strong_results.json');a=ap.parse_args()
    r=run(a.n,a.noise,a.seed)
    with open(a.output,'w') as f:json.dump(r,f,indent=2)
    print({k:(r[k]['selected'],r[k]['n']) for k in ['null_one_body','hidden_two_body']})
--- END EMBEDDED garden_candidate026_strong_comparator.py ---

4.9.3 INVARIANT / CONSTRAINT REGISTER
These equations are imported established science or scoped author-formulated models. Laws, reductions, constitutive relationships, empirical models, safety obligations and economic conditions are distinct. The complete input/parameter dimensions are retained in code; the following entries expose equations, domains, assumptions and falsifiers. General equations received scoped manual review; there is no claim that every law has an executable physical oracle or automatic symbolic units proof.
MASS | EXACT_BALANCE | dm_cv/dt = sum(mdot_in)-sum(mdot_out)
  Domain: nonrelativistic control volume with reaction species tracked; assumptions: No unmodelled mass ports; chemical changes preserve total mass to engineering precision
  Dimensions: m:kg;mdot:kg/s;t:s; required variables: mass_flow,material_mass,time
  Falsifier: Residual exceeds combined measurement/model tolerance after all ports included; sources: PHY-THERMO
ENERGY | EXACT_BALANCE | dE_cv/dt = Qdot-Wdot+sum(mdot*(h+v^2/2+g*z))_in-out
  Domain: classical control volume, consistent enthalpy convention; assumptions: All material, electrical, radiative and mechanical ports counted; signed terms
  Dimensions: E:J;Qdot:W;Wdot:W;h:J/kg;v:m/s;g:m/s^2;z:m; required variables: energy,time
  Falsifier: Energy residual cannot be explained by uncertainty or omitted storage/ports; sources: PHY-THERMO
CHARGE | EXACT_BALANCE | dQ_cv/dt = - integral_surface(J dot n dA)
  Domain: electrical control volume; assumptions: Conduction and charge accumulation distinguished
  Dimensions: Q:C;J:A/m^2;A:m^2;t:s; required variables: current,time
  Falsifier: Nonzero continuity residual with sufficient spatial/time resolution; sources: PHY-ELECTRIC
KCL | CONDITIONAL_REDUCTION | sum(I_branch)=0
  Domain: lumped electrical node; assumptions: Charge storage at node negligible on selected time scale
  Dimensions: I:A; required variables: current,topology
  Falsifier: Unmodelled capacitance makes branch-current sum measurably nonzero; sources: PHY-ELECTRIC
OHM | CONSTITUTIVE | V=I*R(T); R=R0*(1+alpha*(T-T0))
  Domain: ohmic conductor in calibrated temperature band; assumptions: Geometry fixed; linear temperature coefficient valid locally; skin effects included in effective R if relevant
  Dimensions: V:V;I:A;R:ohm;alpha:1/K;T:K; required variables: voltage,current,resistance,temperature
  Falsifier: Measured V/I or temperature coefficient outside contract tolerance; sources: PHY-ELECTRIC
JOULE | CONSTITUTIVE | P_loss=I_rms^2*R_ac
  Domain: passive resistive element; assumptions: R_ac includes waveform/frequency effects; constant R during averaging window
  Dimensions: P_loss:W;I_rms:A;R_ac:ohm; required variables: current,resistance
  Falsifier: Energy measurement inconsistent with frequency-aware resistance model; sources: PHY-ELECTRIC
ACPOWER | CONDITIONAL_REDUCTION | P=3*V_phase_rms*I_phase_rms*cos(phi)
  Domain: balanced sinusoidal three-phase system; assumptions: No significant harmonics or phase imbalance; phase voltage convention
  Dimensions: P:W;V_phase_rms:V;I_phase_rms:A;phi:rad; required variables: voltage,current,phase
  Falsifier: Harmonic or imbalance contributions change real power materially; sources: PHY-ELECTRIC
FARADAY | EXACT_FIELD_LAW | emf=-d(lambda_B)/dt
  Domain: stationary circuit path; moving-path emf needs motional term; assumptions: lambda_B is total oriented flux linkage, equal to N*Phi_B for N identical turns
  Dimensions: emf:V;lambda_B:Wb;t:s; required variables: flux,time
  Falsifier: Flux/emf mismatch after motional and parasitic terms resolved; sources: PHY-ELECTRIC
TORQUE | CONDITIONAL_BALANCE | J_rot*domega/dt=tau_drive-tau_load-tau_loss
  Domain: rigid rotating shaft equivalent; assumptions: Equivalent inertia and shaft coupling applicable; torsional modes omitted
  Dimensions: J_rot:kg*m^2;omega:1/s;tau:N*m;t:s; required variables: inertia,speed,torque,time
  Falsifier: Torsional or variable-inertia effects exceed error budget; sources: PHY-ELECTRIC
SHAFT | KINEMATIC_POWER | P_shaft=tau*omega
  Domain: rotational power port; assumptions: Consistent signed torque and angular velocity
  Dimensions: P_shaft:W;tau:N*m;omega:1/s; required variables: torque,speed
  Falsifier: Input/output mechanical energy fails balanced accounting; sources: PHY-ELECTRIC
HEAT | CONSTITUTIVE | Qdot_cond=-k*A*dT/dx; Qdot_conv=h*A*(T-Ta)
  Domain: Fourier conduction and boundary convective closure; assumptions: Local material/flow coefficients calibrated; continuum; no universal h
  Dimensions: Qdot:W;k:W/(m*K);A:m^2;h:W/(m^2*K);T:K;x:m; required variables: temperature,ambient_temperature,thermal_conductance
  Falsifier: Independent temperature/flux profile disagrees outside tolerance; sources: PHY-HEAT
RAD | CONSTITUTIVE | Qdot_rad=epsilon*sigma*A*(T^4-Tsur^4)
  Domain: gray diffuse exchange with large isothermal surroundings; assumptions: View factor one or explicitly absorbed into model; Kelvin temperatures
  Dimensions: Qdot:W;epsilon:1;sigma:W/(m^2*K^4);A:m^2;T:K; required variables: temperature,ambient_temperature,emissivity
  Falsifier: Geometry/spectral effects create material systematic residual; sources: PHY-HEAT
THERMAL | CONDITIONAL_BALANCE | C*dT/dt=P_in+Q_abs-Q_loss
  Domain: lumped thermal state; assumptions: Biot/time-scale validity tested; positive C; all sources/sinks counted
  Dimensions: C:J/K;T:K;t:s;P_in:W;Q_abs:W;Q_loss:W; required variables: temperature,thermal_capacity,heat_power,cooling_power,time
  Falsifier: Persistent internal gradients defeat single-temperature closure; sources: PHY-HEAT,PHY-LINE
ENTROPY | SECOND_LAW_CONSTRAINT | dS_cv/dt=sum(Qdot_j/T_j)+Sdot_mass+Sdot_gen; Sdot_gen>=0
  Domain: thermodynamic control volume; assumptions: Reservoir temperatures positive; entropy ports and composition known
  Dimensions: S:J/K;Qdot:W;T:K;Sdot:W/K; required variables: temperature,heat_power
  Falsifier: Inferred negative production beyond uncertainty indicates model/data error; sources: PHY-THERMO
HYDRO | CONDITIONAL_POWER | P_hydraulic=rho*g*Qvol*H
  Domain: incompressible pump/turbine useful hydraulic head; assumptions: Head definition includes consistent boundary velocities and pressures
  Dimensions: rho:kg/m^3;g:m/s^2;Qvol:m^3/s;H:m;P:W; required variables: water_flow,head
  Falsifier: Measured energy balance exceeds applicable efficiency/loss model; sources: PHY-PUMP,PHY-THERMO
PV | EMPIRICAL_MODEL | P_dc approx P_ref*(G/G_ref)*(1+gamma*(Tcell-Tref))
  Domain: PV array near calibrated irradiance and temperature envelope; assumptions: No clipping, mismatch or low-light extrapolation; DC operating point near MPP
  Dimensions: P:W;G:W/m^2;gamma:1/K;T:K; required variables: irradiance,cell_temperature
  Falsifier: Independent irradiance/temperature/IV data exceeds prediction interval; sources: PHY-PV
EFFICIENCY | ENGINEERING_ACCOUNTING | P_out=eta*P_in; 0<=eta<=1
  Domain: single useful-energy output passive converter; assumptions: Steady or storage-corrected averaging interval; other energy inputs excluded only when demonstrably absent; heat-pump COP distinct
  Dimensions: P:W;eta:1; required variables: efficiency
  Falsifier: Measured greater-than-one eta signals additional input or boundary error; sources: PHY-THERMO
TRANSFORMER | CONDITIONAL_REDUCTION | V2/V1=N2/N1; P1=P2+P_copper+P_core+dE_field/dt
  Domain: transformer in calibrated operating envelope; assumptions: Ideal ratio is approximation; flux saturation and leakage qualified separately
  Dimensions: V:V;N:1;P:W;E_field:J;t:s; required variables: voltage,current,flux
  Falsifier: Harmonics/saturation/leakage materially violate ideal relation; sources: PHY-ELECTRIC
SAG | CONDITIONAL_MECHANICS | sag approx w*L_span^2/(8*H_tension)
  Domain: level-span parabolic conductor approximation; assumptions: Uniform load; small sag/span; tension depends on temperature, creep and installed length
  Dimensions: sag:m;w:N/m;L_span:m;H_tension:N; required variables: span_length,tension
  Falsifier: Catenary or unequal supports materially change clearance result; sources: PHY-LINE-SAG
CLEARANCE | ENGINEERING_CONSTRAINT | min_x(clearance(x,t))>=clearance_required
  Domain: overhead line profile with terrain and crossing context; assumptions: Required value from applicable asset/jurisdiction owner; value UNKNOWN here
  Dimensions: clearance:m;t:s; required variables: clearance
  Falsifier: Survey/model predicts or measures clearance below sourced requirement; sources: PHY-LINE-SAG
THERMAL_LIMIT | ENGINEERING_CONSTRAINT | max_x(T(x,t))<=T_limit(component,condition,duration)
  Domain: asset operating envelope; assumptions: Limit requires manufacturer/material/inspection basis; not inferred from average T
  Dimensions: T:K;t:s; required variables: temperature,time
  Falsifier: Hotspot exceeds limit or limit lacks applicable support; sources: PHY-LINE
SWITCH_ENERGY | CONDITIONAL_BALANCE | E_L=0.5*L*I^2; E_C=0.5*C_e*V^2
  Domain: linear inductance/capacitance during switching; assumptions: Magnetic saturation and nonlinear capacitance absent or represented
  Dimensions: E:J;L:H;I:A;C_e:F;V:V; required variables: inductance,current,capacitance,voltage
  Falsifier: Transient energy/voltage cannot be accounted for within model; sources: PHY-ELECTRIC
STORAGE | ENGINEERING_ACCOUNTING | dE_store/dt=eta_c*P_charge-P_discharge/eta_d-P_self
  Domain: bounded storage system; assumptions: Efficiencies and self-discharge calibrated; energy definition fixed
  Dimensions: E_store:J;P:W;eta:1;t:s; required variables: stored_energy,efficiency,time
  Falsifier: Metered energy closure error beyond measurement tolerance; sources: PHY-ELECTRIC,PHY-THERMO
BATTERY_CHARGE | CONDITIONAL_BALANCE | dSOC/dt=eta_coul*I/Q_cap
  Domain: coulomb-counting battery state estimator; assumptions: Current sign defined charging positive; usable capacity age/temperature dependent
  Dimensions: SOC:1;I:A;Q_cap:C;t:s; required variables: state_of_charge,charge_capacity,current,time
  Falsifier: Reference capacity measurement or side reactions invalidate estimator; sources: PHY-ELECTRIC
CAPACITY | ENGINEERING_CONSTRAINT | 0<=E_store<=E_usable(condition); abs(P)<=P_limit(condition)
  Domain: qualified storage operating domain; assumptions: Age, temperature and SOC bounds from applicable data; no universal numeric limits
  Dimensions: E_store:J;P:W; required variables: stored_energy,usable_capacity,power_limit
  Falsifier: Observed/predicted state violates asset-qualified bound; sources: PHY-ELECTRIC
PUMP_SYSTEM | CONSTITUTIVE_SYSTEM_MODEL | H_pump(Qvol,N)=H_static+K*Qvol^2
  Domain: steady pump and piping intersection; assumptions: Single-phase; calibrated resistance; static head retained; NPSH checked separately
  Dimensions: H:m;Qvol:m^3/s;N:1/s;K:s^2/m^5; required variables: water_flow,head,speed
  Falsifier: Measured operating point not explained by curves or cavitation occurs; sources: PHY-PUMP
HEAT_RECOVERY | ENGINEERING_ACCOUNTING | Qdot_recovered<=Qdot_available; DeltaT_approach>0
  Domain: passive heat exchanger without heat-pump work; assumptions: Source/sink time, temperature, flow and fouling qualified
  Dimensions: Qdot:W;DeltaT:K; required variables: heat_power,temperature,service_temperature
  Falsifier: Claimed duty exceeds energy balance or violates temperature pinch; sources: PHY-HEAT,PHY-THERMO
DEGRADATION | EMPIRICAL_MODEL | D(t)=integral(rate(T,stress,chemistry,history),dt)
  Domain: damage state only within calibrated mechanism; assumptions: Rate and accumulation rule must be externally identified; no universal Miner/Arrhenius validity
  Dimensions: D:1;rate:1/s;t:s; required variables: damage_state,time,temperature_history
  Falsifier: Held-out degradation or recovery invalidates rate/accumulation model; sources: PHY-LINE-AGE
ISOLATION | SAFETY_REQUIREMENT | E_residual<=E_safe(context) and isolation_verified
  Domain: maintenance/decommissioning effect boundary; assumptions: Actual switching authority and lockout procedure outside research simulator; threshold UNKNOWN
  Dimensions: E_residual:J;E_safe:J; required variables: residual_energy,isolation_state
  Falsifier: Residual live source or stored energy defeats safe-state claim; sources: PHY-ELECTRIC
RECOVERY_MASS | ENGINEERING_ACCOUNTING | m_feed=m_products+m_rejects+m_emissions+Delta_m_process
  Domain: recycling batch with moisture and reagents tracked; assumptions: All material additions counted; composition balance separately required
  Dimensions: m:kg; required variables: material_mass,recovered_mass,reject_mass
  Falsifier: Weighed/assayed mass balance fails beyond uncertainty; sources: PHY-THERMO
MATERIAL_QUALITY | ENGINEERING_CONSTRAINT | composition_product in customer_spec and measured_properties in acceptance_set
  Domain: recycled material reuse; assumptions: No universal purity requirement assumed; specification and test values UNKNOWN
  Dimensions: composition:1;property:typed_per_property; required variables: material_composition,material_quality
  Falsifier: Product fails declared functional acceptance tests; sources: PHY-THERMO
All numeric manufacturer limits, measurement uncertainties, protection settings, degradation parameters and applicability to a particular asset remain UNKNOWN unless supplied separately. In an open control volume, conservation requires flux and storage accounting; subsystem energy is not assumed constant.

4.9.4 TRANSITION AND COMPOSITION AUDIT
Initial interface validation exposed 170 unbound-name diagnostics, largely from conflating state observations with physical flow ports. Physical review introduced explicit state-port ownership and corrected actual routing mistakes: added AC/DC charging conversion; separated metering information from charge power; removed fictitious inverter DC-output supply; separated breaker state from ordinary bus current; routed recovered materials to repair/manufacturing. This is a repaired modelling audit, not blind scientific discovery. No deliberately missing transfer was planted in the primary graph.
Each listed transition now has a declared matching type/direction/unit. Numeric equalities, identity of physical assets, common timestamps, transformation ratios and conservation remain obligations. Feedback and lifecycle edges do not imply instantaneous chronology.

CONNECTED-PAIR AUDIT
All 90 incoming-edge pairs are enumerated with an explicit same-consumer structural witness. Shared binding names are flagged rather than added blindly. This does not evaluate all 90 joint physical models. The primary executed compositional witness is harmonic-source aggregation: two separate 100 A h5 sources can cancel to 0 A or reinforce to 200 A depending on relative phase. Phase-free source summaries match, while calculated harmonic loss differs by 441.74338991 W. Therefore harmonic phasors must be aggregated at the relevant terminal before the scalar spectral moments are formed. This is established circuit physics, retained as a negative case against over-generalizing the repair.

4.9.5 RANKED SCIENTIFIC GAPS AND PRIOR ART
Ranking is research priority by identifiable incremental cost/value and testability, not a fabricated forecast of positive return. Every economic claim must use equal delivered service, risk, information access, interventions and resource budgets. E0=no economic measurement; E1=synthetic scenario; E2=independent physical measurements; E3=controlled deployment economics. No E2/E3 evidence exists here.
1 / C6 | Consumer-model update -> retained harmonic summary -> downstream decision
  Gap: New model may require information erased by old summary. Revalidate with model-qualified loss bounds and request additional data only for ambiguous decisions.
  Strong comparator: Conventional kernel-aware moment LP; edge scalar loss query; task-aware/event-triggered compression.
  Economic status: Net marginal communication, compute, storage, delay and deferment cost per update. Synthetic LP E1; measured money E0. Novelty of narrow integration UNKNOWN; matched conventional LP ties.
2 / C1 | Converter/charger waveform -> transformer heat -> dispatch
  Gap: RMS+THD can omit frequency-weighted heat; preserve relevant moments and aggregate phasors before summarizing.
  Strong comparator: Full-spectrum harmonic transformer-loss model, modern ageing/Volt-VAr models.
  Economic status: Loss energy at identical useful service plus actual implementation costs. E1 known-mechanism simulation; incremental Garden operating value versus matched full-spectrum model exactly 0.
3 / C2 | Conductor cooling -> connector heating -> allowable loading
  Gap: Local connector resistance and heat transfer can bind despite cool spans; narrow low-cost calibration question remains open.
  Strong comparator: 2025 coupled conductor-clamp thermal model with laboratory calibration.
  Economic status: Congestion/curtailment avoided minus sensors, maintenance and unchanged reliability cost. E0; broad physics known.
4 / C3 | Cable heat -> soil water migration -> later cable rating
  Gap: Thermal/hydraulic state and wetting/drying history may be lost at the dispatch interface.
  Strong comparator: 2018 cycling cable/soil model; 2026 coupled thermal soil/ampacity model.
  Economic status: Safe scheduled-energy gains net of sensing, model upkeep and failure cost. E0; broad coupling known; no simplified model superiority claim.
5 / C4 | Thermal rating -> protection/mechanical constraints -> redispatch
  Gap: A thermal-safe action need not satisfy joint relay, clearance, stability and contingency requirements.
  Strong comparator: DOE DLR guidance; modern transient-temperature AC optimal power flow plus explicit protection/clearance contracts.
  Economic status: Earlier/cheaper detection of omitted operating constraints, with equal dispatch feasibility. E0; intersection of constraints known.
6 / C5 | Weak thermal excitation -> parameter estimate -> claimed capacity
  Gap: Inverse estimation becomes poorly conditioned near weak temperature/current contrast; point estimates may hide uncertainty.
  Strong comparator: Uncertainty-aware conductor-temperature/parameter estimation.
  Economic status: Avoided unnecessary derating minus sensing/identification expense at fixed reliability. E0; broad ill-conditioning known.

4.9.6 EQUATION CONTRACTS, DERIVATIONS AND QUANTITATIVE TESTS
E1 COMPLETED SIMULATION AND MATHEMATICAL NOTES — 2026-10-09
NONCANONICAL. Known physical model; synthetic parameters; no real-data validation.

Protocol: experiment_memo.txt SHA-256
19984e724c2453b83f78694c0c65333e629c2da0cfd976fee2bde84122a1fa71
This was written before implementing and running the experiment, but is a LOCAL
freeze and not independent preregistration. Later adversarial API validation fixes
did not change the parameters or frozen physical predictions.
Reproduce: python electricity_experiment.py (Python standard library only).

RESULTS
At fundamental current 950 A, the h5 and h13 cases both have RMS 968.813707583 A
and THD 20%. Whole-transformer losses are 4512.29840939 W and 6094.34068752 W.
Difference 1582.04227812 W agrees with the separate analytic expression to
6.83e-13 W. Synthetic steady temperature proxies are 96.4037301174 C and
116.179258594 C. The proxy threshold is 105 C. Maximum fundamental currents
under the same thermal/current limits are 1038.84928923 A and 862.589679732 A.
These are model outputs, NOT permissible real-transformer operating ratings.

The RMS-only and assumed-h5 interfaces select the zero-auxiliary h13 mode and
violate the actual declared proxy constraint. The conservative h13 interface
returns no admissible mode at 950 A. The repaired moment interface and existing
full-spectrum model choose exactly the same admissible h5 action.

For 200 fixed-seed randomized spectra, maximum full-spectrum/moment loss
difference is 1.82e-12 W. On 200 randomized fundamental current levels, decisions
between the SAME FIXED TWO spectral modes match 200/200. These are two different
checks; do not describe them as 200 new optimization problems with randomized
mode spectra. Zero-harmonic, zero-frequency-dependent-loss, zero-current,
out-of-domain, negative-loss, zero-load-coefficient and infeasible-core tests pass.
Period quadrature and Parseval RMS agree to relative error 2.49e-16.
Electrical/thermal balance checks are constructed bookkeeping identities; they
do not independently validate the physical equations or conservation in hardware.

MATHEMATICAL CLOSURE CRITERION (ESTABLISHED LINEAR ALGEBRA)
Let x_h=(I_h/I_ref)^2 >=0, and y=A x be retained linear spectral moments. A required
linear output q=k^T x can be reconstructed for every nonnegative x if and only if
k belongs to row(A), assuming the full nonnegative cone is the declared domain.
Sufficiency: if k=A^T beta then q=beta^T y.
Necessity: if k is outside row(A), there exists z in ker(A) with k^T z !=0.
Write z=z_plus-z_minus with both parts nonnegative. Then A z_plus=A z_minus
but k^T z_plus != k^T z_minus. This is an exact closure-failure witness.
On a more restricted physically attainable set, row membership is sufficient
but need not be necessary; admissibility of the pair must be checked separately.

For the declared transformer loss law, rows [1,h^2,h^.8] make total heat loss
recoverable for every coefficient triple (D,E,S), not just the chosen constants.
For a downstream quantity with a different harmonic coefficient vector, the
same moments may be insufficient. Do not infer safety, voltage, ageing or
dynamic closure from this single heat-loss output closure.

If a candidate reconstruction has residual r=k-A^T beta, then
 |q-beta^T y|=|r^T x| <= ||r||_infinity * ||x||_1.
All x entries are dimensionless, k and r have watts, so the result has watts.
Applying the bound to harmonic entries only uses M_harm=sum_(h>1)x_h.
Under the steady thermal proxy, absolute temperature error is at most
 ||r||_infinity*M_harm/G [K]. A common feasible action set must first be made
robust to the bound before the familiar two-times-cost-error regret bound
can be used. This experiment derives the bound; it does NOT implement or test
a robust controller, and does not independently establish a real residual bound.

SECOND-CYCLE FALSIFIER
Two terminal spectra with fundamental 1000 A and h=5,7,11,13 harmonics were
constructed from an exact matrix-nullspace cofactor formula. Their M0,M2,M.8
agree within 2.67e-15. Declared-model losses differ by <9.10e-13 W.
Under the deliberately artificial added coefficient 5000*(h/13)^1.5 W for
h>1, the omitted losses are 120.497892273 W and 120.962541091 W. Thus the same
retained moments now correspond to output losses differing by 0.464648818 W.
Each omission lies below the derived bound 200 W; thermal error bound 2.5 K.
Response: invalidate exact sufficiency for the changed constitutive law. A new
independently justified coefficient-error bound or revised full model is needed.
The artificial residual law is NOT a discovered physical correction.

Aggregation counterexample: two separate 100 A h5 sources have identical
per-source phase-free summaries. Their aggregate h5 current is 0 A when phases
oppose and 200 A when aligned. This changes model harmonic loss by 441.74338991 W.
Therefore phasors must be aggregated BEFORE computing terminal moments. An
interface carrying moments of separate devices without phase cannot generally
close transformer losses. This limitation is established power-quality physics.

DIMENSIONS AND TYPE SEPARATION
Current I [A]; voltage V [V]; h and M_p [1]; P0,D,E,S,L [W]; G [W/K]; temperature
differences [K]; hours [h]; prices [currency/kWh]; annual value [currency/year].
Psource=Puse+L+Paux is a conservation bookkeeping equation.
L=P0+D*M0+E*M2+S*M.8 is an approximate constitutive relationship.
G*(theta-Ta)=L is a synthetic steady thermal approximation.
theta<=105 C and Irms<=1200 A are assumed engineering operating constraints.
Positive net scenario value is an economic decision condition.
None of these distinctions grants fundamental-invariant status to all equations.

ECONOMICS
At fundamental current 800 A both modes satisfy the same proxy constraints and
deliver 554256.258422 W useful service. High-/low-order transformer losses are
4612.60724655/3490.71576954 W. Low-order mode assumes 200 W extra auxiliary loss.
Net energy saving is 921.891477006 W. At assumed 8000 h/year and price 0.10/kWh,
saved energy is 7375.13181605 kWh/year and gross value 737.513181605/year.
After assumed 1200 capital cost over five years at zero discount and 50/year
maintenance, conditional annual net value is 447.513181605. An actual converter
capable of these two modes and these costs has NOT been identified or tested.
Do not combine this scenario with lifespan, safety, capacity or industry savings.
The existing full-spectrum model achieves the IDENTICAL outcome. Incremental
Garden operating value relative to that strong model is zero; any extra Garden
deployment cost makes the incremental net negative absent some separate benefit.

PRIOR ART AND GATES
Senol, Bayram, Shi 2025 accepted manuscript (equations 5,7,8) gives the harmonic
loss weighting; its more complete transformer ageing model is NOT implemented
here. The synthetic linear steady thermal proxy is deliberately scoped.
https://strathprints.strath.ac.uk/92564/1/Senol-etal-IEEE-TECE-2025-Transformer-aging-under-harmonic-emissions-from-electric-vehicle.pdf
Liu et al. 2016 documents limits of the h^2 eddy approximation and corrected
frequency behavior, so noticing that approximation limits compression is known.
https://ietresearch.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1049/iet-epa.2015.0559
Physical/artifact control feasibility: UNKNOWN.
Full contemporary transformer safety/ageing comparison: NOT_RUN.
Independent empirical test: NOT_RUN.
Frontier novelty: NOT_ESTABLISHED.
Measured incremental economic benefit: UNKNOWN.
Blinded matched-budget Garden discovery advantage: NOT_TESTED.
Result retained as a useful known-physics closure and falsification benchmark.

E2 — MODEL-QUALIFIED INTERVAL REVALIDATION
Physical link: terminal current waveform -> retained spectral features -> changed transformer-loss kernel -> thermal proxy decision. State x_h=(I_h/I_ref)^2 >=0 is dimensionless; h is integer harmonic order in a finite declared band. Observations y=A*x have row-declared dimensions. Response q=k^T*x is W when k is W. P0 is a separately qualified fixed W offset; changing voltage/core physics reopens that assumption. For fixed exact y and supplied kernel k, calculate L=min(k^T*x), U=max(k^T*x), subject to A*x=y,x>=0. An all-one row fixes total spectral energy and makes the feasible set bounded. Dependent-row profiles return UNKNOWN in this v1; budget exhaustion returns INCOMPLETE. Empty feasible set returns INFEASIBLE, never a vacuous safety pass.
Upper certificate: A^T*lambda>=k gives k^T*x<=lambda^T*y. A feasible x attaining that value and the dual inequality certify the exact rational optimum. Lower certificate reverses the inequality. Both certificates are checked against caller-supplied trusted A,y,k; matching model names or a digest alone cannot qualify them. A certificate proves a bound for supplied inputs, not their authenticity, empirical applicability or safe actuation.
Decision: U<=B -> MODEL_FEASIBLE; L>B -> MODEL_INFEASIBLE; otherwise NEED_ADDITIONAL_MEASUREMENT. B is the remaining qualified heat budget [W]. Uncertainty extension: for measured intervals l<=A*x<=u and fixed upper dual lambda, U_outer=sum(lambda_i*u_i if lambda_i>=0 else lambda_i*l_i). A separately justified coefficient error ||delta k||inf<=epsilon adds epsilon*M0_max [W]. Lower bound uses the opposite interval endpoint and subtracts epsilon*M0_max. This uses known LP/dual-norm mathematics. The interval helper handles trusted internal certificates; the public qualification path does not claim robust external packet deployment. Quantization, sensor calibration, time alignment, support escape and proof of kernel-error limits remain open.
Implemented solver: exact Fraction arithmetic with finite basis enumeration, not a scalable general-purpose optimiser. Maximum default 10,000 bases. Necessity challenge: a specific full-waveform-retrieval prerequisite is unnecessary when retained features exactly reconstruct the needed quantity or bound it on one side of the decision. It remains necessary to obtain more information for ambiguous decisions. This is a counterexample within a declared finite profile, not a claim that waveform recording is generally unnecessary.
Frozen E2 workload: 100 seeded synthetic spectra, 8 harmonic bins, old three retained features and a small model-kernel change. Result: 86 MODEL_FEASIBLE, 14 MODEL_INFEASIBLE; 0 incorrect certified decisions against full spectrum; 0 ambiguous cases. This workload lay away from the decision boundary. An additional explicitly post-freeze adversarial witness has interval [39440,40160] W and threshold 39560 W; it correctly returns NEED_ADDITIONAL_MEASUREMENT. The boundary witness is not blended into the frozen sample score.
E2 deterministic result-row SHA256: f43ba113b67aea4620ae7ba65e7a13c1157b9afff1b2c06a1f963a28a2b208ad. Local runtime approximately 0.592 seconds for this run; runtime is not a stable reproducibility fingerprint.
Illustrative marginal payload after old features were already stored: interval reuse 0 new bytes across this far-boundary workload; always-scalar-query baseline 3,200 bytes; always-full-spectrum baseline 8,800 bytes (8-byte values plus assumed 24-byte response header). Conventional kernel-aware LP also uses 0 new bytes. These are packet-accounting assumptions, not a measured network saving, and exact-rational proofs have not been qualified for binary64 wire encoding. Offline LP compute, old-summary acquisition/storage, coefficients, update metadata, availability of historical raw data, latency and ambiguity costs can reverse the cost comparison. Single-consumer edge scalar query is the relevant cheap baseline, not only full-spectrum transmission.

4.9.7 ECONOMIC ASSESSMENT AND FALSIFIERS
Use Incremental annual net = affected annual baseline cost * eligible fraction * independently measured improvement * adoption - annual operating costs - annualized implementation costs, only when those factors refer to disjoint, consistently scoped quantities. The relevant baseline is a strong existing method. Do not count energy saving twice as both avoided losses and purchased-energy reduction. No industry total is estimated here.
E1 conditional energy scenario: 7,375.131816 kWh/year saved under hypothetical waveform-control modes; at 0.10 currency/kWh, 737.513182 gross/year less 290 annualized costs = 447.513182 net/year. The strongest full-spectrum model selects the same action and earns exactly the same amount. Therefore Garden-specific incremental operating value is 0; additional Garden costs would make it negative unless a separate benefit is measured. Waveform controllability and costs remain unverified.
E2 measurable target: total cost per model update = communication + compute + retained-state storage + delayed/declined service + integration/maintenance. Candidate Delta_net = C_strong_baseline - C_candidate at identical decision reliability and service. Break-even requires avoided-query/retention/revalidation cost to exceed exact/approximate solver, proof, telemetry and integration cost. No positive net estimate is currently grounded. A predeclared >20% marginal-byte target is met only in the constructed far-boundary workload; this is not a net-cost or field result.
Next falsifiable narrow hypothesis: on previously unseen real transformer model-update logs, a model-qualified revalidation layer reduces total per-update cost versus scalar-query, conventional kernel-aware LP and established task-aware compression while preserving decision outcomes under independently calibrated uncertainty. Reject the superiority claim if net benefit is nonpositive, field bounds are violated, ambiguous-case rate offsets savings, or a comparator achieves equal/better performance. Freeze hardware, tariff/latency, model-update distribution, sensor errors, support and action constraints before evaluation. All novelty, field value and methodological superiority gates remain UNKNOWN.

4.9.8 EXECUTED VALIDATION, NEGATIVE FINDINGS AND NEXT FRONTIER
Integrated suite: 18 tests passed, including an optional SciPy HiGHS independent-algorithm test. The harness itself uses Python standard library only. Persisted tests check graph/source bindings, malformed/UNKNOWN preservation, missing consumer inputs, units/source resolution, fixed-point bounds, exact algebra, LP primal/dual certificates, uncertainty corners, forged/stale model receipts and physical nulls. The optional 200-profile LP comparison has 195 supported exact cases agreeing with HiGHS (maximum reviewer-observed error 1.60e-14), with five dependent-row profiles explicitly UNKNOWN.
Separate adversarial role additionally checked 500 algebra matrices using determinant minors (189 closed, 311 nonclosed, all agreed); 96 supported interval-error profiles against independent robust LP bounds, with four dependent profiles excluded as UNKNOWN; and physical/API counterexamples. Its review found and prompted repairs rather than simply approving the proposer. Role separation shares model family and pretraining: it is not independent scientific certification. A retrospective or seeded test does not establish blind discovery advantage.
Retained negative findings: broad harmonic loss, conductor-clamp coupling, moisture/ampacity feedback, joint dispatch constraints and inverse-identification conditioning are known; three-moment sufficiency fails after out-of-span kernel changes; individual-device moments fail under unknown phasor aggregation; weak models can profit by violating omitted limits; exact certificates fail when detached from actual inputs; more named situations do not establish completeness; packet reduction alone does not establish money saved.
Next useful electricity expansions are bounded by material interface questions: validate C6 on actual historical model changes and measurement errors; expand converter harmonic aggregation into network impedance/resonance and phase-aware consumer kernels; qualify connector/cable interfaces against the cited coupled models; resolve externally sourced inputs in E20/E28/E33/E38/E44/E77 before promising device-level predictions. The unexpanded branches listed above remain open. We have not tested or ruled out all potential electricity innovations.
No recurring or unattended background process was started. Reproducible active-session cycles are implemented; continued work needs an active execution session or a separately configured workflow. No paid provider calls, grid commands, purchases, or canonical edits were made.
